# Slice: global-skills (the user's ~/.claude/skills, and the global hooks)

Built from nine part files in `parts/` (appended in full below the summary), each read line by line by a worker; my own part is `small-skills-hooks.md`. Method rows total about 950: COVERED ~320, PARTLY ~300, MISSING ~310 (counts per part in the Read section). Machine-ledger rows (machines-a/b) are mostly per-machine data and the kit is right not to hold them.

## Read
- synth-re: SKILL.md, method.md, starting-a-machine.md, toolchain.md, verification.md, archive.md, scripts (docstrings; setup-re-tools.sh in full), playbook-full.md 13545-end (parts/synth-re-core.md: 78 rows, 25/29/24). engine-lessons.md 1-1785 and worked-examples.md 1-885 (parts/engine-lessons.md: 126 rows, 49/43/34). panel-and-ux.md 1-2987 and panel-from-the-real-unit SKILL.md 1-369 (parts/panel.md: 123 rows, 44/34/40). machines.md 1-3250 (parts/machines-a.md: 157, 62/42/53) and 3250-6427 (parts/machines-b.md: 178, 44/56/78). midi-law.md (byte-identical in synth-re and tempo-sync) 1-667, tempo-sync 1-302, firmware-recon 1-317, patch-decode 1-266 (parts/recon-decode-sync.md: 70 rows, 14/26/30). synth-design-law incl. style guide, preset-design-law incl. three references, synth-diagrams and scripts (parts/design-laws.md: 81 rows, 28/21/32; echofold diagram lines ~631-872 checked by schema only). build-a-unit, patch-generator (matrix12.json lines 1-80 of 3463), patches-database (parts/patches-build.md: 52 rows, 23/18/11). taste-skill 1-1208, image-to-code 1-1230, redesign 1-180, output 1-51 (parts/ui-skills.md: 58 rows, 24/18/16). new-project, latest-versions, shell-rules, workflow-bugs in full; other global skills; global hooks and settings (parts/small-skills-hooks.md: 26 rows).
- Not read: ~/.claude/skills/.trash (161 timestamped folders, deleted skills, not listed by name; worth one look by the audit owner), ~/.claude/skills/synced (claude.ai built-ins).
- Fact found: `sync-from-playbook.py` splits only sections 1-25, so playbook section 26 (soft-synth measuring rig) exists only in playbook-full.md. The Mac copies of gnu-shell/shell-traps/version-pin-guard in ~/.claude are older than the kit's global/hooks.

## Other global skills (one line each) and hooks
See parts/small-skills-hooks.md. Short form: agent-jobs bears on builds (model tiers, 6-agent cap, queue; partly in kit); advanced-skill-writer, brandkit, devto-writing, kanopy-deploy, machine-identity, operator, pi-delegate, profiles, reddit-apify-scraper, reddit-writing, synced, tim-capabilities, tim-writing do not bear on building synths/plugins/audio/UI. Hooks that help builds and live only here: agent-model (enforces model on every Agent spawn), agent-log (per-agent time/token ledger), log-check (Stop hook forcing a log when a fault is admitted), overlap and settle in ~/.claude/bin; gnu-shell, shell-traps, version-pin-guard are in the kit. build-guard.py, settled-guard.py, statusbar-activity.cjs are present but not registered.

## Top gaps (ten, ranked, for an unattended build from firmware and photos)
1. Decode technique set absent: corpus byte profile, find the record, constraint search, builders, read the DSP, scaling, flag bits, id-as-label-index, closure test as proof of a layout (synth-re 5.x, patch-decode, machines-b row 33).
2. A firmware-recon skill: dump triage (stuck address lines, wrong size, interleave, entropy), CPU identification by census and vendor-name trap, base scoring with margin, vector tables, timer discovery (firmware-recon, machines-a rows 11, 12, 63, 108, 119, 120, 130).
3. Disassembler scripts and per-CPU traps (dis6301, dis8051, mcs48dasm, extract_dsp, setup-re-tools, eprom-lookup) not in re-toolchain.
4. Soft-synth measuring rig (section 26) and the filter/parameter measurement lessons (switch-sized values, wide-open normalisation, second independent quantity) into measure-a-law (it is six lines).
5. Default UX shell as a spec, and the reference-photo grade, aspect table, mode-key questions for panels (panel rows 1-4, 7-12, 92-98).
6. Tempo: the SYNC-INT-default law versus the kit's off-by-default rule (needs a decision), plus rhythm-vs-timbre, slew of synced times, two clocks, tick table.
7. workflow-bugs skill (dangling kit reference) and log-check Stop hook; agent-model enforcement and per-agent ledger.
8. Bank diversity and coverage: ledger with percentile refusal, farthest-point pick, hash seeds, per-control coverage gate with `needs`, N x N probe for choice controls; effects units routing profiles.
9. output-skill: a check against elided code and a deliverable-count line in the workorder template.
10. synth-diagrams: generated strict diagrams with a currency gate and regenerate-and-stage pre-commit hook; and the "other makers' presets are statistics only, nothing ships" licence rule.

## 3. Verdict: synth-re

NOT FULLY IN THE KIT: PARTLY. The kit holds the discipline (two sources, provenance tags, firmware out of git, names-first, source ledger, tripwires, test-off-the-easy-point, meter-before-engine, clock probe, shell/gates) but not the decoding technique, nor the soft-synth measuring rig, nor the per-CPU lessons. synth-re is `disable-model-invocation: true` and user-invoked only, so disabling it costs nothing while the kit holds the content; it is NOT safe to disable until these move:
1. Section 26 (playbook-full.md lines 13552-13672): move 26a-26f into sound-design/skills/measure-a-law (neutral values, isolation set, wrong plugin, excitation, Q, carrier).
2. Method 5.1-5.9 and 8a: a decode skill set (profile-the-corpus, find-the-record with constraint search, trace-the-builders, read-the-dsp incl. host-bootstrap hunt) plus the C-idioms and dead-ends lists.
3. Section 13: per-CPU disassembler traps and descent/coverage/base-scoring lessons into the library `toolchain/disassemblers` READMEs; the scripts dis6301.py, dis8051.py, mcs48dasm.py, extract_dsp.py, setup-re-tools.sh, eprom-lookup.py into the library/kit tools.
4. 14b `static`/thread_local grep, 14e read-the-tests, 14f interface refusal, REF/DS tags.
5. Archive lookup and write-back habit (eprom-lookup, re-notes.md, cpu-overrides).
Not carried by design: sync-from-playbook.py and archive.md's per-machine grade tables (per-machine); machines.md, panel-and-ux.md, engine-lessons.md, midi-law.md, worked-examples.md are other workers' files.

| 122 | A sibling's patch corpus run against your map is [COR] evidence (single-byte params only, below the divergence offset; find how far apart the models are first) | WE same | M | Run a sibling's bulk dump against the firmware-derived map, single-byte parameters below the divergence point. | reverse-engineering/skills/bank-census |
| 123 | Vendor conventions: 7-to-8 bit packing, ASCII name at offset 0 space-padded, fixed record size from name stride, offset-64 bipolar, sync flag beside division index; Korg bit-packs several parameters per byte (a byte-per-parameter assumption round-trips perfectly and means nothing; range not spanning the byte is the tell) | WE "What Roland and Korg did the SAME way" | P: midi/rules/sysex-is-a-record ("7-bit packing... right length with every byte wrong", "bit fields"); the five conventions and the Korg bit-pack tell absent | Add the conventions list and the "descriptor range under the byte's span" tell. | midi/rules/sysex-is-a-record or reverse-engineering/skills |
| 124 | Roland SysEx: F0 41 dev model cmd addr data sum F7; DT1=12, RQ1=11; checksum covers address+data only with `& 0x7F`; address is 7 bits per byte, not flat; split data over 256 stitched by address; model ID 1/2/4 bytes; 3 vs 4 byte address | WE "The Roland SysEx message, once" and "Two encodings" | P: bounce-a-plugin has DT1 render options; no checksum or address-septet law found by grep | Put the DT1 frame, checksum mask and 7-bit address rule in the sysex rule. | midi/rules/sysex-is-a-record |
| 125 | Nibble splitting: a value goes out as two bytes; an address map stepping by 2 is the tell; address high byte mirrors panel section | WE "Two encodings and a layout rule, from the JU-06" | M | Recognise a stride-2 address map as nibble splitting. | midi/rules/sysex-is-a-record |
| 126 | Cheapest route when hardware exists: move a control, watch the wire; files may be plain ASCII (open and look before assuming a format) | WE "Three more things about Roland" | M | Open the file and look before assuming a format; with hardware, record the wire while moving each control. | reverse-engineering/skills/gather-sources |

## Counts
126 rows. COVERED: 49. PARTLY: 43. MISSING: 34 (by the leading label of the coverage column).

## Top gaps (ranked, for an unattended synth build from firmware and photos)
1. Rows 44-49, 60-61: filter and parameter MEASUREMENT method (switch-sized values, divide by wide-open render, clamped reference, source-subtract band slope, noise is not white, second independent quantity, ratio against own reference); measure-a-law is 6 lines.
2. Rows 74-75, 78: tempo model details: rhythm vs timbre (FREE slot), per-sample slew of synced times, jump-only re-anchor, median-filtered pulses, lock release.
3. Row 119, 116, 120, 107, 109: table-finding tools: width-prefixed packed run sweep, symbol tick tables, always-zero-byte sequence signature, fingerprint of consecutive maxima, RTOS task table.
4. Rows 90-91, 88, 93: effect KIND definitions (phaser/flanger/chorus/comb/delay), delay fed from input, builder + branch-free loop, gap-depth delay-vs-reverb measure.
5. Rows 2-3, 12, 27: engine mistakes: full modulation matrix, stored/live/selector classes, per-program RMS probe + inspector + build stamp, model envelope shapes as they are.
6. Rows 121-125: sibling model (structure not enum values), vendor conventions, Roland DT1 checksum and 7-bit address, nibble splitting.
7. Rows 71, 70, 64: sync discovery traps: audio clock vs tempo clock, hardware vs plugin answers as two rows, a working knob is not a wired parameter.
8. Row 98, 99: layers share one filter and one envelope; wave entries carry generation method and above-Nyquist behaviour.
9. Row 16, 15: partial-commit message form; stated build order and freezing parameter IDs at step one.
10. Row 53, 112, 62: [EMU] tag, weak [COR] when corpus never varies, predict-then-measure.

# PART: panel

# Kit audit part: panel (panel-from-the-real-unit + synth-re panel-and-ux.md)

## 1. Read

- `~/.claude/skills/panel-from-the-real-unit/SKILL.md`: lines 1-369 (all).
- `~/.claude/skills/synth-re/references/panel-and-ux.md`: lines 1-2987 (all; section 16 at 5-533, section 19 at 535-2987).
- Kit files read to judge coverage: audio-core rules (panel-sizes-by-kind, straight-on-and-aligned, panel-surface-and-sections, knob-anatomy, house-control-behaviours, colour-by-role, panel-gestures, presses-held-for-the-firmware, control-reaches-every-surface, a-control-is-proved-through-its-input, parameter-ids-never-change, every-time-control-syncs, discrete-params-snap); skills new-panel-control, prove-control; instrument-shell rules (all 7) and skills hit-the-painted-control, gates-from-commit-one; base analyse-reference-look and page-matches-reference-look; synth-creator ui-drawn-in-code; reverse-engineering read-a-screen-image; midi clock-law section 5; rebuild.md W2, W13, W14; plus greps over packs, prompts, docs, global.

Context finding: the kit draws the panel IN CODE from a control table and forbids sprites, images and an HTML prototype (`ui-drawn-in-code`, `no-html-prototype`, rebuild.md W13). The synthpanel-creator methods (native export, sprite cache, themes JSON, `panel:native`, bindings.json `kind`) therefore do not carry over as such; rows marked **N/A (design)** are that. Only the lessons under them are counted.

## 3. Verdict: panel-from-the-real-unit

**PARTLY IN THE KIT.** The build-and-prove half is in the kit (rows 5, 10, 15 to 17, 20, 22: `analyse-reference-look`, `page-matches-reference-look`, `a-control-is-proved-through-its-input`, `prove-control`, `hit-the-painted-control`, `gates-from-commit-one`). Missing: the reference-usability grade (rows 1 to 4), the rack aspect constant table and shape audit (7, 8), mode-key questions and a per-control source for its job (11, 12), "faceplate holds only the machine's real controls" (6), and the can-the-user-open-it check (9). Rows 21 gives a source-ranking conflict between rebuild W2.4 and `analyse-reference-look`.

**Safe to disable once the kit holds rows 1 to 4, 6 to 9, 11 and 12 and the row 21 conflict is settled.** Not safe now: it is the only place the photo-quality test, the aspect table and the mode-key checklist live. Its tool names (`synthpanel-creator`, `panel:native`, `dead_controls.py`, `panel_aspect_check.py`) are per-machine and should not be copied; the lessons under them should.

## 4. Counts and top gaps

Rows: 123. COVERED 44, PARTLY 34, MISSING 40, N/A (design) 4, one mixed (row 108: COVERED threading, IDs, sysex; MISSING original numbering).

Top gaps for an unattended build, ranked:
1. Default UX shell as a spec (rows 92 to 98, 109): window layout, performance row, tabs, patch explorer, inspector, build stamp, compare, standalone defaults. The kit has rules about the shell but no spec of what it holds.
2. Reference grade and shape: usable photo tests, no-photo stop, 19-inch aspect table, shape audit (rows 1 to 4, 7, 8).
3. Test-mode counts as a panel checklist (row 25) and the bicolour lamp recipe (23, 24).
4. Mode key and control-job questions with a source per row (11, 12) and 3-position switch kind (46).
5. Audition source rules for effects (70, 71) and arp ownership policy (99, 100, which contradicts rebuild W8).
6. Opening size and viewport numbers: 1512 x 982 minimum, scale formula, fit pass (55, 113).
7. Keyboard: base note per machine, macOS alert, drum map (63, 64).
8. Percent-silence rules: pool overflow refused and counted, import losses counted, scope honesty (75, 80, 116).
9. Source-rank conflict between rebuild W2.4 and `analyse-reference-look` (21).
10. Category list from the machine and two-pass categoriser (89, 95).

## 1. Read

Lines 1-3250 of machines.md, in order, in chunks (1-400, 401-850, 851-1300, 1301-1750, 1751-2200, 2201-2600, 2601-2930, 2931-3250). Nothing skipped. Line 3250 ends inside the Sony DRE-2000 header (its body is in the other worker's range). BRIEF.md read first.

Kit read for coverage: all of `packs/reverse-engineering` (11 rules, 11 skills, 5 checks, tools), `packs/sound-design` (rules, skills, checks), `packs/midi` (rules, `does-it-sync`, `clock-probe` + `clock-law.md`), `packs/base` skills, `prompts/workorder/templates/rebuild.md` (W0-W14), plus grep over packs, prompts, docs, global.

Short names used below: RE = `packs/reverse-engineering`; R-two = `RE/rules/two-sources-before-naming.md`; R-neg = `RE/rules/a-negative-names-what-was-scanned.md`; R-addr = `RE/rules/addresses-name-their-space.md`; R-law = `RE/rules/every-law-has-a-source.md`; R-wd = `RE/rules/findings-are-withdrawn-in-place.md`; R-acct = `RE/rules/account-every-term.md`; R-fw = `RE/rules/firmware-stays-out.md`; R-repro = `RE/rules/reproduction-or-original.md`; S-names = `RE/skills/names-first`; S-gather = `RE/skills/gather-sources`; S-menu = `RE/skills/menu-map`; S-tool = `RE/skills/re-toolchain`; SD = `packs/sound-design`; W# = step of `prompts/workorder/templates/rebuild.md`. Every "kit holds no firmware-triage tool or skill" below was checked: no `dump_triage`, autocorrelation, interleave, checksum-convention, hash-dedupe or k-mer diff anywhere in packs/prompts/docs/global.

## 2. Methods

| # | method | source (machines.md line, quote) | kit coverage | what the kit should gain | where it belongs |

## 3. Machine-knowledge ledgers

The file holds per-machine ledgers (data, not methods), all under `## 12` of `~/projects/eproms/reverse-engineer.md`: Ensoniq ESQ-1, Mirage; Roland JP-8000, D-50, SDE-330/SRV-330, SRV-2000, DEP-5; Oberheim OB-Xa, OB-8; TC Reverb 4000, 2290, Triple-C, M-ONE; Lexicon PCM 90/91, PCM 80, earlier units, PCM 60, PCM 70, 224, M300, family pass; Access Virus B/C; Eventide H3000, H3500; Yamaha REV7, REV5, SPX90; Sony DPS-D7, DRE-2000; Korg DRV-1000; AMS RMX 16. Plus a modulation-survey ranking (AN1x for rotary, DLS-300) and an open-items list per machine.

The kit holds none of them (checked: no machine address, table offset or ledger of any of these in `packs/`, `prompts/`, `docs/`, `global/`). That is correct: a per-machine ledger is data. It belongs with the machine, outside the kit: in each machine's own repo (`docs/research.md`, `docs/laws.md`, `todo.md`) and the archive/eproms project's `reverse-engineer.md`, with the reference library holding a per-machine "what is known" index. The kit should hold only the PROCESS for keeping one: provenance tags (R-law), withdrawals in place (R-wd), the open-items-with-leads list (`re-deliverables`). A new user of the kit starts every machine from zero, unless the reference library ships such an index; that is a data question, not a kit gap.

One naming/evidence convention the kit lacks (see rows 28 and 113): tags `[EMU]`, `[DS]`, `[REF]`, `[SCH]`-vs-`[DS-REF]` and the tier word (Tier-2) are in the ledger's vocabulary; R-law lists seven tags only.

## 3. Counts

Counted from the Methods table (row 179 is a merge marker and is not counted; rows 39 and 178 are noted merges but counted by their label):

Rows 1-178: COVERED 44, PARTLY 56, MISSING 78 (row 57 holds a stray pipe in its text and is MISSING). Many rows are the same method seen on several machines and are merged on purpose; roughly 20 are close duplicates (38/39, 140/33, 178/143), so about 155 distinct methods.

Tensions with the kit worth a decision: (a) names-first says "Look for storage offsets in a panel or menu table" is a mistake, but the DX7 descriptor table at 0xF551 gave (max, offset) for every byte (row 38). (b) a-neg says count agreement is ONE source, but the ledger treats "three counts on one enumeration" as confirmation (row 98, 136). (c) Row 1 (call targets on instruction starts) is valid on a linear sweep and self-fulfilling on recursive descent (row 6).

## 4. Top gaps (PARTLY or MISSING, ranked for an unattended build from firmware and photos)

1. Row 33: arithmetic closure test (sums, tilings, chain links, widths = bytes, counts including holes) as the proof of any layout. The ledger's single strongest confirmation habit; the kit has nothing.
2. Rows 43, 44, 48: statistical detectors with controls: per-column entropy over strides and phases, pointer-base enrichment vs shifted bases, name-index agreement vs shift and shuffle. Add as RE tools next to names-sweep.
3. Rows 8-12, 15, 53: a firmware-recon skill in the kit (CPU identification by census in both orders and positional measures, vector-table index stubs, interleave then re-run the signature, reset path and relocation, DSP code inside the host ROM, no disassembler for an unidentified ISA).
4. Rows 1-5, 125, 126: base scoring with a metric, a sweep and a margin; data-base scoring; mirrors; "a run in data is not a table" and "split a DSP container".
5. Rows 123, 119, 120, 122, 19: word-stream strings sweep, container laws (length and checksum n of n), the vendor's updater as a source, open every file regardless of extension.
6. Rows 80, 81, 27, 28, 29: read the firmware's own display routines, packer and transmit handler instead of fitting; check what a display can print.
7. Rows 65, 66, 67, 68, 69: clock pinning from independent readings, timer-mode trap (8254 mode 3 halves the period), control-rate derivation, pitch reference 440 vs 442, counter quantisation.
8. Rows 59, 60, 61, 62, 63: sample-ROM method: census program vs waves, chip pairing and ordering discriminators, decoding-candidate test with controls, loop-closure rate fit, disks first on samplers.
9. Rows 86, 22, 41: revision diffing (nearest pair, identical regions, similarity labels), md5/part-number search across the archive, "where do the FACTORY sounds live".
10. Rows 101, 102, 149, 145: filter modes as tap mixes, an inferred topology must predict stated numbers, negative controls go stale, and the [REF] tag with the knob-sweep capture.

# Audit part: firmware-recon, patch-decode, tempo-sync (+ midi-law.md)

## 1. Read

- ~/.claude/skills/firmware-recon/SKILL.md lines 1-317 (all)
- ~/.claude/skills/patch-decode/SKILL.md lines 1-266 (all)
- ~/.claude/skills/tempo-sync/SKILL.md lines 1-302 (all)
- ~/.claude/skills/tempo-sync/references/midi-law.md lines 1-667 (all; the synth-re copy is byte-identical per the brief, not re-read)
- Kit read in full: packs/reverse-engineering/skills/{names-first,gather-sources,source-checklist,re-toolchain,menu-map,bank-census,tripwire-the-law,re-deliverables}/SKILL.md, all 11 rules in packs/reverse-engineering/rules, packs/reverse-engineering/tools/lib/unwrap.mts (header), packs/midi/skills/{clock-probe/SKILL.md, clock-probe/clock-law.md 1-417, does-it-sync/SKILL.md}, packs/midi/rules/*.md (5), prompts/workorder/templates/rebuild.md (grep). Greps over packs/, prompts/, docs/ for each method keyword.

Note: kit `clock-law.md` is a trimmed copy of midi-law.md. It LACKS midi-law sections 0 (two clocks, hardware vs ours), the stored-tick-table/row-by-row/appendix/glyph block of 3, the SOURCE state and its four questions, the stopped-host/one-switch/no-clock block of 5, the two-state-switch/no-EXT naming block of 8, and section 10 (synthshell-specific).

## 2. Methods

COVERED = kit holds it. Row ids F = firmware-recon, P = patch-decode, T = tempo-sync / midi-law.

| # | method | source (file + quote/heading) | kit coverage | what the kit should gain | where it belongs |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Run a dump triage before any decoding: entropy per window (flat 8.000 = encrypted; 7.98-7.999 = compressed), count compression magics against what random data gives | firmware-recon "0. TRIAGE THE DUMP FIRST" | PARTLY: `unwrap.mts` has `entropyOf`, names-first step 3 says "text is rarely encrypted"; no triage step, no chance-baseline for magics | Run a triage on every image before names-sweep: entropy per window, magics against chance, and say encrypted or compressed plainly. | new tool `dump-triage` in packs/reverse-engineering/tools, called from `names-first` step 0 |
| F2 | Minimum period of each half: a repeating dump means undriven address lines; fit nothing to the repeat (PCM 60: 256 real bytes in 4096) | same, "Minimum period of each half" | MISSING | Find the minimum period of each half and the real size before any fit. | same triage tool; rule `a-negative-names-what-was-scanned` |
| F3 | Stuck address lines: `b[i]==b[i^(1<<k)]` near 100% vs healthy 40-50% | same, "Stuck address lines" | MISSING (names-first only says "scrambled address lines" as a wrapping) | Test every address bit for a stuck line (near 100% match against 40-50%) before trusting a dump as good. | triage tool |
| F4 | Blank half / 2764 half used / 2732 read as 8 KB; no 0x00 byte in 4 KB (1 in 10^7) means not plain 8-bit code | same | MISSING | Flag a constant half and an absent 0x00 byte as structural findings. | triage tool |
| F5 | ROM size / published variation count: exact division = one block per variation (DRV-1000 32768/128=256), not dividing = data is elsewhere (battery RAM) | same, last bullet | MISSING | Divide ROM size by the published variation count; read an exact quotient as a block-per-variation layout, a remainder as data held elsewhere. | `names-first` (use the count, step 7) or triage tool |
| F6 | Look the unit up in the archive before opening it (dumps, manuals, updates, re-notes, recon.md) and grade readiness A to F | firmware-recon "0a" | PARTLY: `gather-sources` step 1 (read the folder) and `source-checklist`; no readiness grade, no per-model recon note | Open each run with a recon note per machine: CPU guess with reason, disassembler, readiness grade A to F. | `gather-sources` |
| F7 | Strings sweep first, and grep it for the thing about to be claimed (`tempo|bpm|sync|midi|clk`, numbered menus); repeat it for the clock, not only the menu | firmware-recon section 1 | COVERED (packs/reverse-engineering/skills/names-first) for names; PARTLY for the clock: the sweep is name-driven from the manual, no rule to sweep for `tempo|bpm|sync|clk` | Add the clock-vocabulary sweep (`tempo|bpm|sync|midi ?clk|clock`) to names-first and cite it from does-it-sync. | `names-first`, `does-it-sync` |

| F9 | No strings can mean WORD-ADDRESSED: sweep again one char per 16-bit word, both endians, when load addresses advance by half the payload | section 1 | PARTLY: `unwrap.mts` has word reversal and 3-bytes-per-word; no one-char-per-word sweep, no "load addresses advance by half" trigger | Add a char-per-word wrapping (LE and BE) to `unwrap` and the trigger to names-first step 3. | `unwrap.mts`, `names-first` |
| F10 | Identify the CPU from the image: reset-vector and boot signatures (x86 far JMP at top, 68000 vector table at 0, 0xFFFE reset for 6502/6809), confirm against the part number, keep an overrides file | section 2 | MISSING (re-toolchain starts from the named processors, never from the image) | Identify the CPU from reset vectors and boot code and confirm it against the part number before choosing a disassembler. | `re-toolchain` step 1 |
| F11 | Vendor-name trap: a manufacturer is not an architecture (NEC uPD7810 vs V55, 78K/III, uCOM-4); a wrong decoder does not error | section 2 | MISSING (two-sources-before-naming says "name a CPU from its own manual", which is the same spirit but not this lesson) | Pick a disassembler by the part's own manual, never by the vendor word; a wrong decoder prints plausible fiction. | rule `two-sources-before-naming` or `re-toolchain` Never-do |
| F12 | Use the house disassemblers (14 listed by CPU); extend them there; MAME unidasm / capstone for the rest | section 3 | COVERED (rule house-tools-live-in-the-library, skill re-toolchain, check disassembler-in-the-library); the per-CPU table itself lives in the user's machine library, not the kit | none, if the library's README lists the 14 | |
| F13 | ARM Cortex-M: capstone Thumb or `llvm-objdump --triple=thumbv7m`; read the vector table (word0 SP, word1 reset, all handlers odd, populated fault vectors 4-6 mean M3/M4) | section 3 | MISSING | Add the Cortex-M recipe (vector table reading, Thumb mode, M3/M4 vs M0 tell) to the toolchain notes. | `re-toolchain` (a processor-notes file) |
| F14 | TI C2000/C28x: no disassembler anywhere; TMS320 is a product line not an architecture; get the memory map by grepping `MOVW DP,#` (0x761F + page x 64) | section 3 | MISSING | Record "no tool exists" processors with their no-disassembler memory-map trick. | `re-toolchain` processor notes |
| F15 | A linear dump from a self-picked address is fiction (desyncs silently); decode only from a proven boundary (reset vectors, call targets, recursive walk) | section 3b habit 1 | PARTLY: addresses-name-their-space ("it disassembles is not proof of code: count undecodable words against a baseline") | Decode only from a boundary reached from a vector or a call target; check an address is in the proven set before trusting a listing. | rule `addresses-name-their-space` |

## 3. Verdicts

- **firmware-recon: PARTLY IN THE KIT.** Names, wrappings, interleave, house-tool placement, two-sources, provenance and render-not-OCR are in. MISSING: dump triage (F1-F5), CPU identification and the vendor-name trap (F10, F11), Cortex-M and C2000 notes (F13, F14), proven-boundary and encoding-counting (F15-F16), timer discovery (F19), DSP carve (F22), analysis order (F24). Not safe to disable until those land.
- **patch-decode: PARTLY IN THE KIT (thin).** Names-first covers stride, count and range-over-printed; nearly all of the decode method is absent: id-as-label-index, signatures and switch test, per-parameter scaling, flag bits, ratio proof, bank-wide attribution, byte-sum "done" (P1, P3, P6-P8, P10-P19). Already `disable-model-invocation: true`, but the content is not elsewhere; not safe to retire.
- **tempo-sync: PARTLY IN THE KIT.** The clock law (midi-law sections 1-9a) is mostly in `clock-law.md` and the midi rules. MISSING: SYNC-INT-default law and its probe (T1, T2, with a conflict against the kit's off-by-default rule), two clocks, hardware-vs-ours, fourth state, tick table, row-by-row list, stopped host, one switch, and the whole firmware-time section (timer, note twin, flag, ratio, scaling). Not safe to disable.

## 4. Top gaps (ranked)

1. T1: default-on SYNC INT law and the "on when it opens" probe; kit `sync-is-a-mode` currently says the opposite.
2. F1-F5: dump triage tool (entropy, repeat period, stuck address lines, blank half, variation-count division).
3. P14 and P15: per-parameter scaling by published-range-at-both-ends, and flag bits inside value bytes.
4. T15: the fourth state (unit as clock source) and "ask what it sends".
5. F19: find the timer and its tick before attributing any time value.
6. P8, P10, P11: id as label-table index; byte identification by signature; inert-sweep means switch.
7. F10, F11: identify the CPU from the image; a manufacturer is not an architecture.
8. T3, T6, T11, T12: two clocks, hardware vs ours, tick table in ROM, row-by-row division list.
9. F15, F16: decode only from a proven boundary; count instruction encodings instead.
10. P3, P13: what a bulk dump covers; range-check each field across the whole bank.

## 3. Verdicts

- **synth-design-law: PARTLY.** The house look, knob anatomy, colour roles, sizes, gestures, primary display, per-instance sync, kill switch, three links and provenance tags are in the kit (audio-core rules, new-panel-control, prove-control). Missing: shell defaults (top bar, band, one arp engine, slot), the N x N matrix for choice controls, layout rules, panel accessibility and colour-vision checks, the legibility viewport probe, echo and action feedback details, tempo extras (fresh chord, bypass keeps clock, swing scope), the palette bands, the Class A/B split, and a single panel review list. Safe to disable once rows 7, 11, 12, 16, 17, 18, 22, 35, 38 are added; the sprite pipeline rows are deliberately not wanted.
- **preset-design-law: PARTLY.** The universal laws, pad/lead/pluck/bass/arp numbers, delay and reverb laws, recipe cards, naming and the per-preset gate are in the kit (design-a-preset and prove-a-bank). Missing: the effects-unit method (three layers, routing profiles, player's vs patch's copy), the coverage tool and gate, `needs` per control, effect recipe form, chain laws, spring/freeze/ducked-hall, bank tiers (3/30/100, signature move, demo test), and the Prodigy treatment laws. Safe to disable once rows 46-54, 58, 62, 63 and 65 are in; the Prodigy annex (69-71) is optional style data.
- **synth-diagrams: NOT IN THE KIT.** Generated, strict, cross-checked machine documents, the currency gate with four perturbations, the regenerate-and-stage pre-commit hook and the one-job-per-commit rule have no kit equivalent (chart-first and route-table are design-time and menu-only). Not safe to disable until rows 73-80 are added; its scripts (`install-hook.sh`, `pre-commit`) should move into the kit with their selftest.

## 4. Counts

81 rows: COVERED 28, PARTLY 21, MISSING 32 (row 14 is an unbuilt item in the source, not a method; rows 24 and 37 are low priority because the kit draws in code).

## 5. Top gaps (ranked)

1. Generated, strict, currency-gated machine documents plus the regenerate-and-stage pre-commit hook (rows 73-79): the only way a build's description stays true, and it finds dead parameters.
2. Bank coverage tool and law: distinct values per control, `needs` per control, exempt-with-reason, fail on flat (rows 49-52), with the routing-profile layers for effects units (rows 46-47).
3. N x N matrix for every choice control (row 22): a sweep cannot show two options sound the same.
4. Shell defaults and shell laws: top bar, keyboard band, slot, one arp engine, tempo extras (rows 16-18).
5. Panel accessibility and colour-vision checks, viewing-condition tests (row 38) and the viewport/legibility probe (row 7).
6. Effects-preset method: three sources test, chain laws, spring/freeze/ducked-hall (rows 58, 62, 63).
7. Bank tiers and demo test: 3/30/100, signature move, one-hand 20 s test (row 65).
8. Commit rule: one verified job per commit, message carries the measured change and the wrong turn (row 80).
9. Layout rules: signal left to right, master output rightmost with meter, display top-centre (row 35).
10. Echo and action-feedback details: automation shown dim, hover does not take the display, save flash, section dim (rows 11-12).

## 3. Scripts

| script | does | general or machine-specific | needs Atlas tabula_research / Tim's repos | kit equivalent |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| patches-database/scripts/ask.py | prints research_stats and research_effects_stats for group:category:synth (filter, envelope, mod, lfo, osc, effects, arp, voice); `--local` reads ~/.cache/tabula/modresearch/stats.json | method general, instance Tim's | YES: Atlas db tabula_research via MONGODB_URI_PATCHES (~/.env), or Tabula's local cache; collections built by Tabula's tools/research/ | none as a tool; the kit bakes the output into design-a-preset tables.md / categories/*.md |
| patch-generator/scripts/genpatch.py | the recipe generator and bank ledger (rows 21-28) | general method | YES: reads research_patches on Atlas, no local fallback; saves to generated_patches | none; design-a-preset holds the medians only |
| patch-generator/scripts/atlas_save.py | validates and inserts a generated patch to generated_patches; failing save raises, exit 3; selftest | pattern general (validate before write, fail loudly), target Tim's Atlas | YES | none needed |
| adapters/adapter_matrix12.py | recipe JSON to Matrix-12 .syx (399 bytes) + .m12.json + Atlas doc | machine-specific (Matrix-12) | Needs data/matrix12.json (built by the Matrix-12 repo) and Atlas for the save (`--atlas off` works without) | none (the method in rows 31-34 is general) |
| adapter_matrix12_map.py | oscillators, LFOs, voice mapping with a report row per field | Matrix-12 specific | mapping JSON only | none |
| adapter_matrix12_filter.py | filter mode, key tracking (KEYBD bit vs route), cutoff by measured word law, envelope times | Matrix-12 specific | mapping JSON | none |
| adapter_matrix12_routes.py | matrix slots (20, max 6 per destination), amount curves, VCA1 lowered by amp-mod depth | Matrix-12 specific | mapping JSON | none |
| adapter_matrix12_laws.py | inverts the measured laws (Hz to word, amount curves, ticks, detune) with nearest-value lookup | specific values; the inversion pattern is general | mapping JSON | none |
| data/matrix12.json | the machine's record layout, parameters, name tables, measured laws | per-machine data, built from the Matrix-12 repo's data/params.csv, ROM tables and its engine probe | built by the Matrix-12 repo | none |

## 4. Verdicts

- build-a-unit: PARTLY IN THE KIT. The standing brief, laws-first, panel-from-photo, never-invent, dead-binding and decay-measurement lessons are in rebuild.md, plugin.md, gates-from-commit-one, prove-control, hit-the-painted-control and every-law-has-a-source. Missing: the skeleton generator itself (kit says it ships none), the Enough / Partial / Not-ready readiness gate before a project is created, the bank-names-equal-the-firmware-table gate, the clock-answer-even-when-no item, and the finish audit tool. Safe to disable once those five are added; it is disable-model-invocation already and references eproms/synthkit paths that other users lack.
- patch-generator: PARTLY. The category numbers it was built on are baked into design-a-preset (tables.md, building-blocks.md) and the adapter's report discipline matches imports-count-every-loss and unreachable-is-said. Not in the kit: the recipe method as a written procedure, the bank ledger with percentile threshold and farthest-point pick, hash-derived seeds, the feature-vector definition, the five-status report schema, [ASSUMED]/[MEAS] tags, and the licence rule. The scripts only work with Tim's Atlas; the Matrix-12 adapter is per-machine. Not safe to disable until rows 26-28 and 31-32 are in the kit; the Matrix-12 adapter itself can stay a Tim-only skill.
- patches-database: PARTLY, and mostly NOT ABOUT BUILDS. It is Tim's private research store (Atlas tabula_research) and cannot work for another kit user. What it taught that belongs in the kit: the "statistics only, nothing ships" licence rule (row 38), reproducible-scope evidence convention (row 40), display-is-not-the-engine and measure-never-guess lessons (rows 42-43), host-state quirks (row 44), and mining pitfalls (row 45). The numbers it produced are already baked into design-a-preset. Safe to disable once rows 38, 40 and 42-45 are written into the kit; keep it as a Tim-only skill for the data.

## 1. Read

All four files in full, in order, no skipping:
- ~/.claude/skills/taste-skill/SKILL.md lines 1-1208 (all)
- ~/.claude/skills/image-to-code-skill/SKILL.md lines 1-1230 (all)
- ~/.claude/skills/redesign-skill/SKILL.md lines 1-180 (all)
- ~/.claude/skills/output-skill/SKILL.md lines 1-51 (all)

Kit read: packs/ui-craft/SOURCES.md, all packs/ui-craft/rules/*.md (16), skills/* (9), the copy checks; packs/web/rules (accessibility, web-performance, tailwind-v4, styles-live-in-stylesheets, production-build), web/skills design-direction, design-review, seo-audit; base/skills/analyse-reference-look (+ depth-measure.md), base/rules/depth-is-designed.md, base/skills/start-from-a-workorder step 5; grep over html-ui, gpu-painter, instrument-shell, writing, prompts, docs.

Key fact: packs/ui-craft/SOURCES.md says the kit was already written from taste-skill (its upstream repo, MIT) and image-to-code (upstream repo). So most rows below are COVERED. What the kit did not take: the web-only layout caps, the dials, the design-system map, the block library, the image-generation procedure, and the output-completeness rule.

Bearing on plugin UIs: almost none of these skills is about audio plugins. taste-skill says itself (section 13) it is "Not dashboards, not data tables, not multi-step product UI, not native". A synth panel is built from a photo (panel-from-the-real-unit, analyse-reference-look, gpu-painter), so only the method rows marked "synth: yes" bear on it. Column "web/synth" below.

## 3. Verdicts

- taste-skill: PARTLY. Already taken into packs/ui-craft (SOURCES.md says so) and web/design-direction, design-review. Missing: rows 3, 6, 8 (emphasis, descenders), 14, 16, 18, 19, 24, 27, 31, 32, 35, 36, 37, 38, 52. Most are web-page caps; only rows 24, 36, 38 and 52 touch a plugin panel. Safe to disable once rows 24, 36, 38 (and optionally 16, 18, 19) are in the kit; its dials, block library and GSAP skeletons are intentionally not taken.
- image-to-code-skill: PARTLY. Extraction method and anti-drift are COVERED (build-from-reference, analyse-reference-look, which is stricter). Missing: image generation by the agent (row 42), regenerate-not-crop (row 43), variation engine and section packs (skipped by design). Safe to disable once rows 42 and 43 are added; weakly relevant to synths (only if mock-ups are generated for a synth with no hardware).
- redesign-skill: FULLY IN THE KIT in method (redesign-page + no-generated-look + survives-real-data + pointer-feedback + type-space-colour). Small gaps: rows 52 and 53. Safe to disable now for plugin work; web-page leftovers are rows 53 and 37.
- output-skill: NOT IN THE KIT (rows 56, 57; row 58 is superseded by unattended-runs). It is not about UI at all: it is a general output-completeness rule and is the one relevant to unattended builds. Safe to disable once a no-elided-code check (row 56) and the deliverable-count line (row 57) are in the kit.

## settings.json notes (helps builds / lives only here)
- env CLAUDE_CODE_SHELL=/opt/homebrew/bin/bash and SHELL: makes the Bash tool run bash 5 (GNU PATH). Mac setup, not a kit item; the kit's hooks are written portable.
- statusLine -> the kit's own statusline.mjs (already in the kit).
- skillOverrides, enabledPlugins (typescript-lsp, clangd-lsp, swift-lsp): clangd-lsp helps C++ builds; per-user, kit could mention it in the cpp pack docs only.
- model opus, effort xhigh, skipDangerousModePermissionPrompt: user preferences.
- Hooks registered: SessionStart gnu-shell; PreToolUse Agent agent-model; Write|Edit|Bash version-pin-guard (the kit's copy); Bash shell-traps; * admiral check-signal; SubagentStop agent-log; Stop log-check. NOT registered though present: build-guard.py, settled-guard.py, statusbar-activity.cjs (agent-cap.py is a stub in hooks/removed).

## Per-skill verdicts

- **new-project**: PARTLY. The kit's packs/base/skills/new-project is the successor (same name; two skills named new-project exist, which is a clash while both are enabled). Missing in the kit: "run latest-versions for the whole stack first" (row 3), "never copy another project's folder" (row 2), and the synthkit generator (Tim's repo, stays in his CLAUDE.md). Safe to disable once rows 2 and 3 are added to the kit skill; the kit one is the better of the two otherwise.
- **latest-versions**: FULLY IN THE KIT (global/skills/latest-versions, byte-identical, plus global/hooks/version-pin-guard). The Mac copy IS the kit's copy installed by `global add`; disabling it removes the kit's own install, so keep it enabled unless `global add` re-installs. Not a loss: safe to disable only if the kit user is assumed to run `global add`.
- **shell-rules**: PARTLY, and mostly Tim-machine-specific (zsh/bash 5, Homebrew GNU, New York time, Tabula). The enforceable rules are in the kit as hooks (gnu-shell, shell-traps in global/hooks, no-polling, build-guard). Missing as text: guard-safe edits (row 12), absolute cd in worktrees (row 11). Safe to disable after row 12 is added; the hooks keep working without the skill.
- **workflow-bugs**: NOT IN THE KIT (kit rule hooks-and-gates cites a `workflow-bugs` skill that no pack ships). It is about HOW builds are run and is the process that keeps a long build honest. NOT safe to disable until the kit holds a workflow-bugs skill (row 15, 16) and ideally the fault-logged Stop hook (row 17).

## Other global skills in ~/.claude/skills (ls), one line each: do they bear on building synths, plugins, audio or UI?

- advanced-skill-writer: writes Claude Code skills; helps the kit's authors, not synth builds (kit has pressure-test-skill and skill-writing rule).
- agent-jobs: YES, bears on builds. Agent model tiers, max 6 agents, queue, balance, pause (rows 18-21). Partly in the kit (subagent-dispatch, agents-held, governor); the model-choice table, agent cap method and queue are not.
- brandkit: image-generation prompts for brand boards; not about builds (marketing images).
- build-a-unit: in my other slice (patches-build part).
- devto-writing: writing articles; not about builds.
- firmware-recon, synth-re, patch-decode, panel-from-the-real-unit, synth-design-law, preset-design-law, synth-diagrams, tempo-sync, patch-generator, patches-database, taste-skill, image-to-code-skill, redesign-skill, output-skill: in the other parts.
- kanopy-deploy: deploying at MongoDB on Kanopy; not about synth builds.
- machine-identity: Tim's accounts, tokens, names, where documents go; not about builds, except it governs commit/push identity (Tim-private; must stay global).
- operator: MongoDB Operator driver usage; not synths (web/data stack).
- pi-delegate: hands mechanical work to a local pi agent; bears on how work is dispatched but depends on Tim's local Ollama and scripts; not for the kit.
- profiles (champion, mongodb, ryan, tim .md): personal writing profiles; not about builds.
- reddit-apify-scraper: Reddit scraping recipe; not about builds.
- reddit-writing: Tim's Reddit voice; not about builds (the kit's writing pack is its public-facing successor).
- synced: claude.ai-synced skills (docx, pdf, pptx, xlsx, browser, computer-use, deep-research, skill-creator, morning, docs, import-memory); generic Anthropic skills, not about synth builds. (pdf may help reading manuals; kit has base/pdf-to-md.)
